本测试块涵盖结节病多系统受累、职业与环境异物性肉芽肿、消化内分泌肉芽肿病及肉芽肿性血管炎：结节病系统性病理 (流行病学与HLA-DRB1年轻非裔女性好发、Scadding胸片分期I-IV期与预后、Schaumann小体同心圆板层钙化球囊、Asteroid星状小体星芒样胞浆包涵体、肉芽肿巨噬细胞自主表达1α-羟化酶不依赖PTH生成1,25-(OH)2D3引起高钙血症高尿钙、血清血管紧张素转化酶ACE监测指标、支气管肺泡灌洗BAL CD4+/CD8+比例极度升高>3.5:1反映Th1区室化积聚、Löfgren综合征急性自限性三联征双肺门淋巴结肿大/结节性红斑/踝关节炎良好预后、冻疮样狼疮Lupus pernio面部浸润斑块慢性难治标志、眼结节病前后葡萄膜炎羊脂状沉淀角膜后壁、Heerfordt综合征葡萄膜腮腺热伴面神经Bell麻痹、神经结节病颅底神经受累与中枢尿崩症垂体柄浸润、心脏结节病室间隔受累传导阻滞室速猝死、肾结节病间质性肾炎与肾钙化、系统激素一线治疗与甲氨蝶呤替代)；职业与异物性肉芽肿 (铍中毒Berylliosis航空航天制造业/BeLPT淋巴细胞转化试验阳性非干酪样肉芽肿、硅沉着病Silicosis石英吸入巨噬细胞溶酶体破裂NLRP3活化同心同轴同质化玻璃样纤维结节/偏振光石英双折射/结核易感性暴增30倍/蛋壳样淋巴结钙化、石棉沉着病Asbestosis铁锈小体Ferruginous bodies下叶间质纤维化胸膜斑恶性间皮瘤、煤工尘肺CWP煤斑大块纤维化与Caplan综合征伴类风湿结节、过敏性肺炎Farmer's lung嗜热放线菌发霉干草BAL CD8+占优松散肉芽肿、静脉吸毒滑石粉肉芽肿微血管栓塞偏振光亮结晶肺动脉高压、外科缝线肉芽肿编织异物多核巨细胞反应)；胃肠肝脏内分泌肉芽肿 (克罗恩病Crohn透壁性非干酪样肉芽肿/节段性跳跃病变/鹅卵石黏膜/匍行脂肪/瘘管 vs 溃疡性结肠炎无肉芽肿、原发性胆汁性胆管炎PBC小叶间胆管慢性肉芽肿性破坏florid duct lesion抗线粒体抗体AMA强阳性熊去氧胆酸治疗、亚急性肉芽肿性甲状腺炎de Quervain病毒感染后颈部剧痛滤泡破坏巨细胞吞噬胶质高ESR低摄碘率、Riedel甲状腺炎石样硬固定伴IgG4硬化纤维化、肝肉芽肿鉴别诊断、膀胱原位癌灌注BCG诱发肉芽肿性肝炎、慢性肉芽肿性胃炎、特发性肉芽肿性乳腺炎产后假性乳腺癌、特发性肉芽肿性睾丸炎)；肉芽肿性血管炎与鉴别体系 (巨细胞/颞动脉炎内弹力板断裂多核巨细胞>50岁头痛视力缺失前部缺血性视神经病变AION、高安动脉炎Takayasu主动脉弓及大分支肉芽肿全动脉炎<40岁年轻亚裔女性无脉症、肉芽肿性多血管炎GPA/Wegener上呼吸道鞍鼻穿孔/肺空洞结节/寡免疫坏死新月体肾炎三联征与PR3-ANCA/c-ANCA蛋白酶3、嗜酸性肉芽肿性多血管炎EGPA/Churg-Strauss坏死性肉芽肿伴致密嗜酸粒细胞浸润/严重哮喘/单发多神经炎垂腕垂足与MPO-ANCA/p-ANCA、显微镜下多血管炎MPA无肉芽肿关键鉴别、Cogan综合征非梅毒性角膜基质炎前庭耳蜗功能障碍大血管炎、环状肉芽肿胶原类纤维蛋白坏死周围栅栏状组织细胞伴糖尿病、糖尿病类脂质渐进性坏死胫前萎缩性斑块、类风湿结节伸侧受压处皮下栅栏状纤维蛋白坏死、Melkersson-Rosenthal综合征口唇肉芽肿面肿/皱襞舌/复发面瘫三联征、慢性肉芽肿病终末期继发AA淀粉样变、USMLE全套肉芽肿病理主干鉴别诊断决策树)。

### Question 101: Sarcoidosis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Sarcoidosis is a systemic granulomatous disease characterized by non-caseating epithelioid granulomas, often affecting young African American adults, particularly females, and associated with specific HLA alleles (HLA-DRB1). The underlying mechanism involves an exaggerated CD4+ Th1 immune response to an unknown environmental antigen.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 34-year-old African American female presents to the clinic complaining of progressive shortness of breath, a persistent non-productive cough, fatigue, and intermittent low-grade fevers over the past 6 months. She denies smoking history and reports no significant past medical history. Physical examination reveals bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on auscultation and mild bibasilar crackles. Chest radiography shows prominent, symmetrical bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (BHL) along with diffuse reticulonodular opacities throughout both lung fields. A transbronchial lung biopsy is performed. Microscopic examination reveals discrete, tightly cohesive, non-caseating epithelioid granulomas surrounded by a sparse rim of lymphocytes. Special stains for fungi (GMS) and acid-fast bacilli (Ziehl-Neelsen) are negative. Which of the following pathophysiological mechanisms is most likely responsible for the granuloma formation in this patient?

(A) Activation of macrophages by soluble antigens, leading to fusion and formation of multinucleated giant cells, characteristic of tuberculosis.
(B) Deposition of immune complexes containing rheumatoid factor in synovial joints, leading to pannus formation and cartilage destruction.
(C) Formation of granulomas composed of activated macrophages (epithelioid cells) and lymphocytes, driven by an exaggerated CD4+ Th1 immune response to an unknown environmental antigen, often associated with HLA-DRB1 alleles.
(D) Direct cytotoxic T-lymphocyte (CTL)-mediated destruction of hepatocytes, resulting in ballooning degeneration and Mallory-Denk bodies, characteristic of alcoholic hepatitis.
(E) Complement-mediated lysis of red blood cells by antibodies directed against RhD antigens, leading to extravascular hemolysis in the spleen.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Demographics:** 34-year-old African American female. This demographic is strongly associated with sarcoidosis, which has a higher prevalence in African Americans and often affects young adults, particularly women.
- **Symptoms:** Progressive shortness of breath, non-productive cough, fatigue, low-grade fevers. These are common constitutional and respiratory symptoms of pulmonary sarcoidosis.
- **Physical Exam:** Bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (auscultation), bibasilar crackles. Hilar lymphadenopathy is a hallmark finding in sarcoidosis. Crackles suggest interstitial lung involvement.
- **Radiography:** Prominent, symmetrical bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (BHL) with diffuse reticulonodular opacities. This radiographic pattern (Stage II sarcoidosis) is highly characteristic of sarcoidosis.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Discrete, tightly cohesive, non-caseating epithelioid granulomas surrounded by a sparse rim of lymphocytes. This is the pathognomonic histological finding for sarcoidosis. The absence of caseous necrosis distinguishes it from tuberculosis.
- **Negative Stains:** Negative fungal (GMS) and acid-fast bacilli (Ziehl-Neelsen) stains rule out common infectious causes of granulomatous disease like histoplasmosis and tuberculosis, respectively.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the pathophysiological mechanism responsible for granuloma formation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
Sarcoidosis is a systemic inflammatory disease characterized by the formation of non-caseating epithelioid granulomas in various organs, most commonly the lungs and lymph nodes. The pathogenesis involves an exaggerated cell-mediated immune response. It is believed to be triggered by an unknown environmental antigen (possibly microbial or chemical) in genetically susceptible individuals. These individuals often carry specific HLA alleles, particularly HLA-DRB1*0301, which is associated with increased susceptibility and a higher risk of chronic disease. The antigen is processed by antigen-presenting cells (APCs), such as macrophages and dendritic cells, which then present it to CD4+ T helper cells. In sarcoidosis, there is an excessive activation of these CD4+ T cells, leading to a predominantly Th1-type immune response. These activated Th1 cells release cytokines, including interferon-gamma (IFN-γ) and tumor necrosis factor-alpha (TNF-α). These cytokines activate macrophages, causing them to transform into epithelioid cells (activated macrophages) and fuse to form multinucleated giant cells (Langhans giant cells). These epithelioid cells, along with surrounding lymphocytes (primarily CD4+ T cells), aggregate to form the characteristic non-caseating granulomas. The lack of caseous necrosis differentiates sarcoidosis granulomas from those seen in tuberculosis. The clinical presentation, radiographic findings (BHL, reticulonodular opacities), and histological findings (non-caseating epithelioid granulomas) in this patient are classic for sarcoidosis, pointing towards this specific immunopathogenic mechanism.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): This describes the pathogenesis of tuberculosis (TB). TB granulomas are characterized by caseous necrosis (a central area of cheesy debris resulting from cell death), which is absent in sarcoidosis. While macrophages and giant cells are involved, the trigger (Mycobacterium tuberculosis) and the presence of caseation are key differences. The vignette explicitly states negative acid-fast stains, ruling out TB.
- (B): This describes the pathogenesis of rheumatoid arthritis (RA). RA involves immune complex deposition (containing rheumatoid factor) in synovial joints, leading to chronic inflammation, pannus formation (proliferation of synovial tissue), and destruction of cartilage and bone. Granulomas are not a feature of RA.
- (D): This describes the pathogenesis of alcoholic hepatitis. It involves direct toxic effects of alcohol and its metabolites (like acetaldehyde) on hepatocytes, leading to hepatocellular injury, inflammation, and characteristic histological features like ballooning degeneration and Mallory-Denk bodies (intracytoplasmic inclusions of keratin filaments). Granulomas are not involved.
- (E): This describes the pathogenesis of hemolytic disease of the fetus and newborn (HDFN) due to Rh incompatibility. It involves maternal antibodies (IgG) against fetal RhD antigens crossing the placenta, binding to fetal red blood cells, and leading to their destruction, primarily in the spleen, via complement activation and phagocytosis by macrophages. Granulomas are not involved.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Sarcoidosis is a systemic granulomatous disease characterized by non-caseating epithelioid granulomas, often affecting young African American females and associated with HLA-DRB1. The underlying mechanism is an exaggerated CD4+ Th1 immune response to an unknown antigen. Key differentials for granulomatous lung disease include tuberculosis (caseating granulomas, positive AFB stain), fungal infections (e.g., histoplasmosis, coccidioidomycosis - often positive fungal stains), and hypersensitivity pneumonitis (poorly formed granulomas, often associated with environmental exposures).

### Question 102: Pulmonary Sarcoidosis Radiographic Staging
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the Scadding radiographic staging system for pulmonary sarcoidosis and its correlation with prognosis (斯卡丁肺结节病放射学分期系统及其与预后的相关性).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old African American woman presents to her primary care physician complaining of a persistent dry cough and shortness of breath for the past 6 months. She denies fever, night sweats, or weight loss. Her past medical history is unremarkable, and she is a non-smoker. Physical examination reveals bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on auscultation, but no other significant findings. A chest X-ray is ordered. The X-ray shows prominent bilateral hilar lymph nodes without any evidence of interstitial lung disease or parenchymal infiltrates. Pulmonary function tests (PFTs) show a restrictive pattern with reduced diffusion capacity (DLCO). A bronchoscopy with transbronchial biopsy reveals non-caseating granulomas, confirming the diagnosis of sarcoidosis. Based on the Scadding radiographic classification system, what stage of pulmonary sarcoidosis does this patient most likely have?

(A) Stage II
(B) Stage III
(C) Stage IV
(D) Stage 0
(E) Stage I

#### Correct Answer: E

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old African American woman. Sarcoidosis has a higher prevalence in African Americans and typically affects young adults.
- **Symptoms:** Persistent dry cough and shortness of breath (dyspnea) for 6 months. These are common respiratory symptoms in sarcoidosis.
- **Negative Symptoms:** No fever, night sweats, or weight loss. This makes infectious causes less likely.
- **Physical Exam:** Bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on auscultation. This is a classic finding in sarcoidosis.
- **Chest X-ray Findings:** Prominent bilateral hilar lymph nodes (BHL) *without* parenchymal infiltrates or interstitial lung disease. This is the crucial finding for staging.
- **PFTs:** Restrictive pattern with reduced DLCO. Consistent with interstitial lung involvement, even if not visible on X-ray, or potentially due to granulomatous involvement of the lung parenchyma or airways.
- **Biopsy:** Non-caseating granulomas. Pathognomonic for sarcoidosis.
- **Question:** Asks for the Scadding radiographic stage based on the X-ray findings.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- The Scadding staging system classifies pulmonary sarcoidosis based on chest radiographic findings.
- **Stage 0:** Normal chest radiograph.
- **Stage I:** Bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (BHL) alone, without parenchymal infiltrates. This stage has the best prognosis, with a high rate (>60%) of spontaneous remission. The patient's X-ray shows BHL without infiltrates, fitting this stage.
- **Stage II:** BHL plus parenchymal infiltrates (typically reticulonodular).
- **Stage III:** Parenchymal infiltrates alone, without BHL.
- **Stage IV:** Pulmonary fibrosis, characterized by honeycombing, traction bronchiectasis, and architectural distortion. This represents end-stage disease.
- The patient's chest X-ray shows only bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (BHL) without parenchymal infiltrates. This precisely matches the definition of Stage I sarcoidosis according to the Scadding classification. The restrictive PFTs and reduced DLCO suggest some degree of lung involvement, but the radiographic stage is determined solely by the presence or absence of BHL and parenchymal infiltrates.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Stage II:** This stage involves both bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (BHL) *and* parenchymal infiltrates. The patient's X-ray shows BHL *without* parenchymal infiltrates. Therefore, this is incorrect.
- (B) **Stage III:** This stage involves parenchymal infiltrates *without* BHL. The patient's X-ray shows BHL *with* the absence of parenchymal infiltrates. Therefore, this is incorrect.
- (C) **Stage IV:** This stage represents end-stage pulmonary fibrosis with irreversible changes like honeycombing and traction bronchiectasis. The patient's X-ray shows lymphadenopathy, not fibrotic changes. Therefore, this is incorrect.
- (D) **Stage 0:** This stage indicates a normal chest radiograph. The patient's X-ray shows prominent bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy. Therefore, this is incorrect.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The Scadding staging system for pulmonary sarcoidosis is based on chest X-ray findings: Stage I (BHL alone), Stage II (BHL + infiltrates), Stage III (infiltrates alone), Stage IV (fibrosis). Stage I has the best prognosis. Key differentials for BHL include lymphoma, tuberculosis, fungal infections, and metastatic disease, but the presence of non-caseating granulomas confirms sarcoidosis.

<a id='question-103'></a>

### Question 103: Sarcoidosis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of characteristic microscopic findings in sarcoidosis, specifically Schaumann bodies (conchoidal bodies) within giant cells, and their composition. (识别结节病中的特征性显微镜下发现，特别是巨细胞内的Schaumann体（ Conchoidal bodies）及其组成。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 36-year-old African American female presents to the clinic with a 3-month history of progressive dyspnea on exertion, non-productive cough, and fatigue. She denies fever, chills, night sweats, or weight loss. Past medical history is unremarkable except for mild seasonal allergies. Physical examination reveals bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on chest radiography. Pulmonary function tests show a restrictive pattern with decreased diffusion capacity. A transbronchial lung biopsy is performed. Microscopic examination reveals multiple non-caseating epithelioid granulomas throughout the lung parenchyma. Within the cytoplasm of several multinucleated Langhans giant cells, distinctive concentric, laminated, basophilic, round-to-oval structures are observed. These structures stain positively for calcium using the Von Kossa stain. What are these structures most likely called?

(A) Asteroid bodies
(B) Michaelis-Gutmann bodies
(C) Mallory bodies
(D) Russell bodies
(E) Negri bodies

#### Correct Answer: (A)

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 36yo African American female. Sarcoidosis has a higher prevalence in African Americans and typically affects young adults.
- **Symptoms:** Dyspnea, cough, fatigue (common respiratory symptoms).
- **Physical Exam/Imaging:** Bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (classic radiographic finding in sarcoidosis).
- **Pulmonary Function Tests:** Restrictive pattern, decreased DLCO (consistent with interstitial lung disease, common in sarcoidosis).
- **Biopsy Findings:** Non-caseating epithelioid granulomas (hallmark histological feature of sarcoidosis).
- **Specific Finding:** Concentric, laminated, basophilic, round-to-oval structures within Langhans giant cells, positive for calcium (Von Kossa stain). This is the key feature pointing to Schaumann bodies.
- **Question:** Identify the name of these specific structures.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Sarcoidosis:** A systemic granulomatous disease of unknown etiology, characterized by the formation of non-caseating epithelioid granulomas in various organs, most commonly the lungs and lymph nodes.
- **Granuloma Formation:** Immune response involving macrophages (epithelioid cells) and T lymphocytes.
- **Giant Cells:** Formed by the fusion of multiple macrophages, often seen in granulomas. Langhans giant cells are a specific type with nuclei arranged peripherally.
- **Inclusion Bodies:** Abnormal structures found within cells. In sarcoidosis, two main types are associated with giant cells:
    - **Schaumann bodies (Conchoidal bodies):** Laminated, concentric, oval or round structures found within the cytoplasm of giant cells. They are composed primarily of calcium carbonate and proteinaceous material. They are not specific to sarcoidosis but are frequently seen. Their presence is often associated with chronic inflammation and granuloma formation. The lamination is due to the deposition of calcium salts in layers.
    - **Asteroid bodies:** Star-shaped, rhomboid-shaped, or needle-like crystalline inclusions found within the cytoplasm of giant cells or epithelioid cells. They are composed of calcium oxalate crystals. They are also seen in sarcoidosis but are considered more specific than Schaumann bodies, although still not pathognomonic.
- **Von Kossa Stain:** A histochemical stain used to detect calcium deposits, which appear black or dark brown. The positive staining of the described structures confirms their calcium content, consistent with Schaumann bodies.
- **The vignette describes:** Laminated, concentric, basophilic (due to protein matrix and calcium salts), round-to-oval structures within giant cells, positive for calcium. This perfectly matches the description of Schaumann bodies.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Asteroid bodies:** These are also found in sarcoidosis giant cells, but they are typically star-shaped or rhomboid and composed of calcium oxalate, not laminated calcium carbonate. The description in the vignette (laminated, concentric, round-to-oval) does not fit asteroid bodies.
- (B) **Michaelis-Gutmann bodies:** These are laminated, basophilic, round inclusions found in the cytoplasm of megaloblasts (immature red blood cells) in pernicious anemia (vitamin B12 deficiency). They represent aggregates of ferritin and mitochondrial remnants. They are found in bone marrow, not lung granulomas or giant cells.
- (C) **Mallory bodies:** These are eosinophilic, amorphous cytoplasmic inclusions found in hepatocytes, typically associated with alcoholic liver disease or other forms of liver injury. They are composed of aggregated intermediate filaments (cytokeratins). They are not found in lung granulomas or giant cells.
- (D) **Russell bodies:** These are eosinophilic, spherical cytoplasmic inclusions found in plasma cells, representing accumulations of immunoglobulins. They are associated with chronic inflammation and plasma cell disorders (e.g., multiple myeloma). They are not typically found within giant cells in sarcoidosis granulomas.
- (E) **Negri bodies:** These are eosinophilic, sharply outlined, intracytoplasmic inclusions found in nerve cells (neurons), specifically in the hippocampus and cerebellum, and are pathognomonic for rabies virus infection. They are not found in lung granulomas or giant cells.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Sarcoidosis is characterized by non-caseating granulomas. Within the giant cells of these granulomas, characteristic inclusion bodies can be found: Schaumann bodies (laminated, calcium carbonate) and Asteroid bodies (star-shaped, calcium oxalate). Remember their morphology and composition.
- **Differentials:** While both Schaumann and Asteroid bodies are seen in sarcoidosis, the description in the vignette specifically points to Schaumann bodies due to the laminated, concentric appearance. Other granulomatous diseases (e.g., tuberculosis, fungal infections) typically feature caseating granulomas, although non-caseating granulomas can occur. Other inclusion bodies have distinct cellular locations and compositions (Michaelis-Gutmann in megaloblasts, Mallory in hepatocytes, Russell in plasma cells, Negri in neurons).

<a id='question-104'></a>

### Question 104: Sarcoidosis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of characteristic microscopic features of sarcoidosis, specifically asteroid bodies within giant cells, and understanding their composition and associated conditions. (识别结节病特征性显微镜下发现，特别是巨细胞内的星状体，并理解其组成和相关疾病。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old African American woman presents to her primary care physician with a 3-month history of progressive shortness of breath, dry cough, and fatigue. She denies fever, chills, night sweats, or weight loss. Her past medical history is unremarkable except for well-controlled hypertension. Physical examination reveals bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on chest radiography. A subsequent mediastinoscopy is performed, and a biopsy of an enlarged mediastinal lymph node is obtained. High-power microscopic examination of the lymph node biopsy shows non-caseating epithelioid granulomas. Within the cytoplasm of several multinucleated Langhans giant cells, distinctive star-shaped, radiating, eosinophilic inclusions are observed. Electron microscopy confirms these inclusions are composed of compressed cytoskeletal elements. Which of the following best describes the composition and significance of these inclusions?

(A) They are composed of calcium deposits and are characteristic of caseating granulomas seen in tuberculosis.
(B) They are composed of aggregated mitochondria and are typically found in granulomas associated with fungal infections like histoplasmosis.
(C) They are composed of compressed cytoskeletal intermediate filaments, microtubules, and centrioles, and are characteristic of non-caseating granulomas seen in sarcoidosis, berylliosis, and foreign body reactions.
(D) They are composed of iron-laden macrophages (hemosiderin) and are characteristic of granulomas associated with chronic inflammation and hemorrhage.
(E) They are composed of laminated concretions of calcium phosphate and are characteristic of dystrophic calcification in areas of necrosis.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 45-year-old African American woman. Sarcoidosis has a higher prevalence in African Americans and typically affects young to middle-aged adults.
- **Symptoms:** Progressive shortness of breath, dry cough, fatigue (common respiratory symptoms in sarcoidosis).
- **Physical Exam/Imaging:** Bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (classic radiographic finding in sarcoidosis).
- **Biopsy Findings:** Mediastinal lymph node biopsy shows non-caseating epithelioid granulomas (hallmark histological feature of sarcoidosis).
- **Microscopic Feature:** Star-shaped, radiating, eosinophilic inclusions within Langhans giant cells (describes asteroid bodies).
- **Electron Microscopy:** Confirms inclusions are composed of compressed cytoskeletal elements (confirms the nature of asteroid bodies).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the composition and significance of these inclusions (asteroid bodies).

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Sarcoidosis:** A systemic inflammatory disease characterized by the formation of non-caseating epithelioid granulomas in various organs, most commonly the lungs and lymph nodes. The etiology is unknown, but it is thought to involve an exaggerated immune response to an unidentified antigen in genetically susceptible individuals.
- **Granuloma Formation:** Activated macrophages (epithelioid cells) aggregate, often fusing to form multinucleated giant cells (like Langhans giant cells). T lymphocytes surround the granuloma.
- **Asteroid Bodies:** These are distinctive inclusions found within the cytoplasm of giant cells in certain types of granulomas. They appear as star-shaped or stellate, radiating, eosinophilic structures on H&E staining.
- **Composition:** Electron microscopy reveals that asteroid bodies are composed of tightly packed, whorled bundles of cytoskeletal intermediate filaments (like vimentin and desmin), microtubules, and centrioles. They represent an abnormal accumulation of these structural components within the giant cell cytoplasm.
- **Significance:** Asteroid bodies are not pathognomonic but are strongly associated with non-caseating granulomas. They are classically described in sarcoidosis, but can also be seen in other granulomatous conditions such as berylliosis (chronic beryllium disease) and foreign body granulomas. They are *not* typically found in caseating granulomas (like tuberculosis) or granulomas associated with certain infections (like histoplasmosis) or hemorrhage.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. Asteroid bodies are associated with *non-caseating* granulomas, not caseating ones. Caseating granulomas, characterized by central necrosis, are typical of tuberculosis and other infections (e.g., fungi, *Nocardia*). Calcium deposits can occur in granulomas, but they are not the primary composition of asteroid bodies, nor are they specific to caseating granulomas.
- (B): Incorrect. Aggregated mitochondria are not the composition of asteroid bodies. While granulomas can occur in fungal infections like histoplasmosis, the characteristic inclusions are typically yeast forms within macrophages, not asteroid bodies.
- (D): Incorrect. Iron-laden macrophages (containing hemosiderin) are called siderophages or heart failure cells (when found in the lungs). They are associated with hemorrhage or iron overload states, not asteroid bodies. While granulomas can sometimes contain hemosiderin, it is not the composition of asteroid bodies.
- (E): Incorrect. Laminated concretions of calcium phosphate are characteristic of dystrophic calcification, which occurs in areas of necrosis (e.g., infarcted tissue, old granulomas). While calcification can occur in sarcoid granulomas, it is a separate process from the formation of asteroid bodies, which are cytoskeletal inclusions within giant cells.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Asteroid bodies are star-shaped eosinophilic inclusions within giant cells, composed of cytoskeletal elements, and are characteristic of non-caseating granulomas, particularly in sarcoidosis, berylliosis, and foreign body reactions.
- **Differentials:** Remember to differentiate asteroid bodies (sarcoidosis, berylliosis, foreign body) from other granuloma types (caseating vs. non-caseating) and other inclusions (mitochondria, calcium deposits, hemosiderin). Also, consider the differential diagnosis for bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (sarcoidosis, lymphoma, tuberculosis, fungal infections, metastatic disease).

<a id='question-105'></a>

### Question 105: Sarcoidosis & Hypercalcemia
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Granulomatous diseases (e.g., sarcoidosis) can cause hypercalcemia via macrophage production of 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D (calcitriol) independent of PTH regulation. (粒状组织疾病（如结节病）可通过巨噬细胞产生 1,25-二羟基维生素 D（降钙素醇），独立于 PTH 调节，从而引起高钙血症。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 38-year-old woman presents to her primary care physician complaining of generalized weakness, constipation, excessive thirst (polydipsia), and frequent urination (polyuria) for the past several weeks. Her past medical history is unremarkable except for a recent diagnosis of sarcoidosis, confirmed by bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on chest X-ray. Vital signs are stable. Physical examination reveals mild muscle weakness. Laboratory investigations show a serum calcium level of 12.6 mg/dL (normal range: 8.5-10.5 mg/dL), elevated 24-hour urine calcium excretion (480 mg/day; normal <400 mg/day), suppressed intact parathyroid hormone (PTH) level of 4 pg/mL (normal range: 10-65 pg/mL), normal 25-hydroxyvitamin D level of 32 ng/mL (normal range: 20-70 ng/mL), and markedly elevated 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D level of 125 pg/mL (normal range: 15-60 pg/mL). A subsequent chest CT scan confirms mediastinal adenopathy consistent with sarcoidosis. Which of the following mechanisms best explains this patient's hypercalcemia?

(A) Increased intestinal calcium absorption mediated by elevated parathyroid hormone (PTH).
(B) Increased bone resorption stimulated by elevated 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D (calcitriol) levels.
(C) Ectopic production of parathyroid hormone-related peptide (PTHrP) by sarcoid granulomas.
(D) Increased renal calcium reabsorption due to elevated 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D (calcitriol) levels.
(E) Autonomous production of 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D (calcitriol) by activated macrophages within sarcoid granulomas.

#### Correct Answer: E

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 38-year-old female with sarcoidosis. Sarcoidosis is a granulomatous disease.
- **Symptoms:** Weakness, constipation, polyuria, polydipsia. These are classic symptoms of hypercalcemia.
- **Lab Findings:**
    - High serum calcium (12.6 mg/dL): Confirms hypercalcemia.
    - High 24-hour urine calcium: Indicates increased calcium excretion, often seen in hypercalcemia due to increased filtered load.
    - Low intact PTH (4 pg/mL): Rules out primary hyperparathyroidism as the cause of hypercalcemia. PTH should be elevated in response to hypercalcemia in primary hyperparathyroidism.
    - Normal 25-hydroxyvitamin D (32 ng/mL): Indicates adequate vitamin D substrate is available.
    - Markedly high 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D (125 pg/mL): This is the active form of vitamin D. Its elevation is the key finding.
- **Imaging:** Chest CT shows mediastinal adenopathy, confirming sarcoidosis.
- **Connecting the Dots:** The patient has sarcoidosis, hypercalcemia, suppressed PTH, normal 25(OH)D, and markedly elevated 1,25(OH)2D. This pattern points towards a non-PTH mediated cause of hypercalcemia involving excessive production of active vitamin D. Granulomatous diseases like sarcoidosis are known to harbor activated macrophages that possess the enzyme 1-alpha-hydroxylase.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Initiating Stimulus:** Sarcoidosis leads to the formation of non-caseating granulomas, primarily in the lungs and lymph nodes (like the mediastinum).
- **Cellular Players:** Within these granulomas, macrophages are activated by various stimuli (e.g., cytokines like IFN-γ).
- **Enzyme Activity:** Activated macrophages express the enzyme 1-alpha-hydroxylase (CYP27B1). This enzyme is normally found primarily in the proximal tubules of the kidney and is tightly regulated by PTH and serum calcium/phosphate levels.
- **Metabolic Pathway:** 1-alpha-hydroxylase converts the inactive precursor 25-hydroxyvitamin D (25(OH)D), produced in the liver, into the active hormone 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D (1,25(OH)2D), also known as calcitriol.
- **Regulation Bypass:** Crucially, in sarcoid granulomas, the activity of macrophage 1-alpha-hydroxylase is *not* subject to the normal negative feedback regulation by PTH and serum calcium.
- **Consequences:** The unregulated production of 1,25(OH)2D leads to:
    - **Increased Intestinal Calcium Absorption:** 1,25(OH)2D increases the expression of calcium transport proteins (e.g., TRPV6, calbindin) in the intestinal epithelium, enhancing calcium absorption from the gut.
    - **Increased Renal Calcium Reabsorption:** 1,25(OH)2D stimulates calcium reabsorption in the distal tubules of the kidney.
    - **Increased Bone Resorption (indirectly):** While 1,25(OH)2D primarily acts on gut and kidney, chronically high levels can also stimulate osteoclast activity, leading to increased bone resorption and release of calcium into the blood.
- **Clinical Manifestation:** The net result is hypercalcemia, which causes the patient's symptoms (weakness, constipation, polyuria, polydipsia) and lab abnormalities (high serum Ca, high urine Ca, suppressed PTH, high 1,25(OH)2D).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Increased intestinal calcium absorption mediated by elevated parathyroid hormone (PTH).** This is incorrect. The patient's PTH level is suppressed (low), not elevated. Elevated PTH *would* increase intestinal calcium absorption, but it's not the mechanism here. This option confuses the cause (PTH) with the effect (intestinal absorption) and ignores the suppressed PTH level.
- (B) **Increased bone resorption stimulated by elevated 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D (calcitriol) levels.** While elevated calcitriol *can* contribute to bone resorption, it is not the primary or initiating mechanism in this context. The primary actions of excess calcitriol are increased intestinal absorption and renal reabsorption. Furthermore, the question asks for the *best* explanation, and the unregulated production of calcitriol by macrophages is the fundamental underlying cause, leading to the downstream effects including potential bone resorption. This option describes a consequence, not the root cause.
- (C) **Ectopic production of parathyroid hormone-related peptide (PTHrP) by sarcoid granulomas.** This is incorrect. Ectopic PTHrP production is a cause of hypercalcemia (e.g., in squamous cell carcinoma), but it is not associated with sarcoidosis. PTHrP mimics PTH action on bone and kidney but not primarily on the intestine, and it would typically lead to suppressed PTH, similar to this case, but the underlying mechanism is different. Sarcoidosis does not cause PTHrP production.
- (D) **Increased renal calcium reabsorption due to elevated 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D (calcitriol) levels.** This is a correct statement – elevated calcitriol *does* increase renal calcium reabsorption. However, it is only *one* of the effects of excess calcitriol and not the *underlying cause* of the elevated calcitriol itself. The question asks for the mechanism explaining the hypercalcemia, which originates from the source of the excess calcitriol. Option (E) describes

<a id='question-106'></a>

### Question 106: Sarcoidosis and Elevated Serum ACE Levels
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Sarcoidosis is a multisystem granulomatous disease characterized by non-caseating granulomas, often associated with elevated serum angiotensin-converting enzyme (ACE) levels due to ACE production by activated macrophages within the granulomas. (沙眼病是一种多系统肉芽肿性疾病，其特征是非干酪样肉芽肿，通常与血清血管紧张素转换酶 (ACE) 水平升高有关，这是肉芽肿内活化的巨噬细胞产生的 ACE 进入血液的结果。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old African American male presents to the clinic complaining of progressive shortness of breath and a persistent dry cough over the past 6 months. He denies fever, chills, night sweats, or weight loss. His past medical history is unremarkable except for hypertension, well-controlled with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on chest X-ray. Pulmonary function tests show a restrictive pattern with decreased diffusion capacity for carbon monoxide (DLCO). A serum angiotensin-converting enzyme (ACE) level is measured and found to be 135 U/L (normal range: 8-52 U/L). A transbronchial lung biopsy is performed, revealing non-caseating epithelioid granulomas. Which of the following mechanisms best explains the elevated serum ACE level in this patient?

(A) Increased production of ACE by endothelial cells in response to chronic inflammation, leading to increased vascular permeability and pulmonary edema.
(B) Increased production of ACE by activated alveolar macrophages within the granulomas, which shed the enzyme into the systemic circulation.
(C) Decreased clearance of ACE by the liver due to granulomatous infiltration of the hepatic sinusoids, resulting in enzyme accumulation in the blood.
(D) Increased production of ACE by malignant cells within the lung parenchyma, contributing to tumor angiogenesis and metastasis.
(E) Increased production of ACE by lymphocytes infiltrating the lung tissue, leading to immune complex formation and deposition in the glomeruli.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 45-year-old African American male. Sarcoidosis has a higher prevalence in African Americans and typically affects young to middle-aged adults.
- **Symptoms:** Progressive shortness of breath, persistent dry cough. Common respiratory symptoms of pulmonary sarcoidosis.
- **Physical Exam/Imaging:** Bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on chest X-ray. Classic finding in sarcoidosis (Stage I or II).
- **Pulmonary Function Tests:** Restrictive pattern, decreased DLCO. Consistent with interstitial lung disease, often seen in sarcoidosis.
- **Lab Finding:** Elevated serum ACE level (135 U/L, normal 8-52 U/L). A known association with sarcoidosis.
- **Biopsy:** Non-caseating epithelioid granulomas. Pathognomonic histological finding for sarcoidosis.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism explaining the elevated serum ACE.

The combination of clinical presentation (respiratory symptoms, hilar lymphadenopathy), PFT findings (restrictive pattern, low DLCO), elevated serum ACE, and biopsy findings (non-caseating granulomas) strongly points to a diagnosis of sarcoidosis. The question specifically targets the reason for the elevated ACE level.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- Sarcoidosis is characterized by the formation of non-caseating granulomas in various organs, most commonly the lungs and lymph nodes.
- These granulomas are composed primarily of activated macrophages (epithelioid histiocytes), T lymphocytes, and multinucleated giant cells.
- Activated macrophages within these granulomas are known to produce and secrete angiotensin-converting enzyme (ACE).
- ACE is an enzyme that converts angiotensin I to angiotensin II, a potent vasoconstrictor, and also breaks down bradykinin.
- In sarcoidosis, the extensive granulomatous inflammation leads to a significant increase in the number of activated macrophages producing ACE.
- This increased production results in the shedding of ACE into the surrounding interstitial fluid and subsequently into the systemic circulation, leading to elevated serum ACE levels.
- While elevated serum ACE is not specific to sarcoidosis (can be seen in other granulomatous diseases like tuberculosis, leprosy, Gaucher disease, silicosis, and conditions like hyperthyroidism, liver disease, and certain malignancies), it is often elevated in sarcoidosis and correlates with the total granuloma burden and disease activity.
- Therefore, the primary mechanism for elevated serum ACE in sarcoidosis is increased production by activated macrophages within the granulomas.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Endothelial cells produce von Willebrand factor and other factors involved in coagulation and vascular permeability, but they are not the primary source of the significantly elevated ACE seen in sarcoidosis. While inflammation can increase vascular permeability, this option misattributes the source of ACE.
- (C): While granulomatous infiltration can affect the liver, the primary mechanism for elevated ACE in sarcoidosis is increased production, not decreased clearance. Liver dysfunction is not the main reason for elevated ACE in this context.
- (D): Malignant cells can produce various substances, but ACE is not typically associated with lung cancer production or metastasis. The biopsy showed non-caseating granulomas, not malignancy.
- (E): Lymphocytes are involved in the immune response in sarcoidosis, but they are not the primary producers of ACE. While immune complexes can form, this option incorrectly identifies lymphocytes as the source of elevated ACE and links it to glomerulonephritis, which is not the primary mechanism for elevated ACE in sarcoidosis.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- High-Yield Takeaway: Elevated serum ACE levels are often associated with sarcoidosis due to increased production by activated macrophages within granulomas. This is a useful, though non-specific, marker for disease activity.
- Differentials: Remember that elevated ACE can occur in other conditions (e.g., tuberculosis, Gaucher disease, hyperthyroidism). The diagnosis of sarcoidosis relies on a combination of clinical, radiological, histological (non-caseating granulomas), and laboratory findings.

<a id='question-107'></a>

### Question 107: Pulmonary Sarcoidosis Diagnosis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Elevated CD4+/CD8+ T-cell ratio (>3.5:1) in bronchoalveolar lavage (BAL) fluid is a characteristic finding in active pulmonary sarcoidosis, reflecting the accumulation of CD4+ Th1 lymphocytes in the lung parenchyma. (BAL CD4+/CD8+ 比值升高 (>3.5:1) 是活动性肺部结节病性肉芽肿的特征性发现，反映了 CD4+ Th1 淋巴细胞在肺实质内的积累。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 30-year-old African American woman presents to the clinic with a 3-month history of progressive dyspnea on exertion and a non-productive cough. She denies fever, chills, night sweats, or weight loss. Past medical history is unremarkable, and she is a non-smoker. Physical examination reveals bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy on chest radiography. Pulmonary function tests show a restrictive pattern with reduced diffusing capacity for carbon monoxide (DLCO). Given the clinical suspicion for sarcoidosis, a flexible bronchoscopy is performed, and bronchoalveolar lavage (BAL) fluid is obtained. Flow cytometric analysis of the BAL fluid shows a total lymphocyte count of 28% (normal < 10%) and a CD4+/CD8+ T-cell ratio of 4.2:1 (normal ~1.5:1 to 2.0:1). Which of the following findings is most consistent with the underlying pathophysiology of this patient's condition?

(A) Increased production of IL-17 by CD4+ T cells, leading to neutrophil recruitment and alveolar damage.
(B) Predominant recruitment of CD8+ cytotoxic T cells into the alveoli, resulting in granuloma formation and fibrosis.
(C) Formation of non-caseating granulomas composed primarily of activated macrophages (epithelioid histiocytes) and CD4+ T lymphocytes within the lung parenchyma.
(D) Deposition of immune complexes containing rheumatoid factor in the alveolar walls, causing inflammation and fibrosis.
(E) Activation of alveolar macrophages by inhaled organic antigens, leading to the release of chemokines that attract CD8+ T cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 30yo African American female (Sarcoidosis has higher prevalence in this demographic).
- **Symptoms:** Progressive dyspnea, non-productive cough (Common respiratory symptoms).
- **Physical Exam/Imaging:** Bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy (Classic finding in sarcoidosis, Stage I or II).
- **PFTs:** Restrictive pattern, reduced DLCO (Suggests interstitial lung disease, consistent with sarcoidosis).
- **BAL Findings:** Elevated total lymphocyte count (28%) and markedly elevated CD4+/CD8+ ratio (4.2:1, >3.5:1) (Highly specific for active pulmonary sarcoidosis).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the underlying pathophysiology consistent with these findings.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- Sarcoidosis is a systemic granulomatous disease of unknown etiology.
- The lungs are the most commonly affected organ.
- The hallmark pathological feature is the presence of non-caseating granulomas.
- These granulomas are primarily composed of activated macrophages (epithelioid histiocytes), multinucleated giant cells (Langhans type), and lymphocytes, predominantly CD4+ T helper cells (Th1 phenotype).
- The elevated CD4+/CD8+ ratio in BAL fluid (>3.5:1) is a highly sensitive and specific indicator of active pulmonary sarcoidosis. This reflects the selective accumulation of CD4+ Th1 lymphocytes within the lung parenchyma, driven by chemokines and adhesion molecules expressed by alveolar macrophages and endothelial cells in response to an unknown antigen.
- These CD4+ T cells release cytokines like IFN-gamma, which further activate macrophages, perpetuating granuloma formation.
- The granulomas can lead to inflammation, fibrosis, and impaired gas exchange, explaining the restrictive PFTs and reduced DLCO.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Increased IL-17 production is characteristic of Th17 cells and is associated with autoimmune diseases like rheumatoid arthritis and psoriasis, as well as some forms of inflammatory lung disease, but not the primary mechanism in sarcoidosis. Sarcoidosis is primarily driven by Th1 cells and IFN-gamma. Neutrophil recruitment is not a major feature.
- (B): While CD8+ T cells are present in sarcoid granulomas, the predominant lymphocyte type is CD4+. A CD8+ predominance (ratio < 1:1) is more characteristic of hypersensitivity pneumonitis or certain viral infections. CD8+ cells are not the primary drivers of granuloma formation in sarcoidosis.
- (D): Deposition of immune complexes containing rheumatoid factor is characteristic of rheumatoid pneumoconiosis or other connective tissue diseases affecting the lungs, not sarcoidosis. Sarcoidosis granulomas are not typically associated with immune complex deposition.
- (E): Activation of alveolar macrophages by inhaled organic antigens leading to CD8+ T cell recruitment is the classic pathophysiology of hypersensitivity pneumonitis (e.g., farmer's lung, bird fancier's lung). Sarcoidosis etiology is unknown, and the characteristic BAL finding is CD4+ predominance, not CD8+.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- High-yield takeaway: Elevated BAL CD4+/CD8+ ratio (>3.5:1) is a key diagnostic marker for active pulmonary sarcoidosis, reflecting the accumulation of CD4+ Th1 lymphocytes driving non-caseating granuloma formation.
- Differentials: Hypersensitivity pneumonitis (BAL CD8+ predominance, ratio < 1:1), Tuberculosis (caseating granulomas, positive AFB stain/culture), Lymphoma (malignant lymphocytes), Fungal infections.

---

<a id='question-108'></a>

### Question 108: Löfgren Syndrome
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Löfgren syndrome is a specific, acute presentation of sarcoidosis characterized by bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy, erythema nodosum, and polyarthritis, typically with a good prognosis. (Löfgren 综合征是结节病的一种特定、急性表现形式，其特征是双侧肺门淋巴结肿大、红斑结节和多关节炎，通常预后良好。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 27-year-old Swedish woman presents to her primary care physician complaining of a 2-week history of fever, severe pain and swelling in both ankles, and painful, warm, erythematous nodules on the anterior aspects of her lower legs. She denies any recent travel, sick contacts, or new medications. Her past medical history is unremarkable. Physical examination reveals a temperature of 38.5°C (101.3°F), bilateral ankle swelling and tenderness, and multiple tender, erythematous nodules consistent with erythema nodosum on both shins. A chest radiograph shows prominent, symmetric bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy without parenchymal infiltrates. A tuberculin skin test (PPD) is negative. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Tuberculosis with erythema nodosum and reactive arthritis
(B) Systemic lupus erythematosus with panniculitis and arthritis
(C) Lyme disease with erythema migrans and arthritis
(D) Reactive arthritis secondary to a gastrointestinal infection
(E) Löfgren syndrome

#### Correct Answer: E

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Demographics:** 27-year-old Scandinavian female. Löfgren syndrome is more common in young adults, particularly women, and has a higher prevalence in Scandinavian countries.
- **Acute Onset Symptoms:** 2-week history of fever, ankle pain/swelling (polyarthritis), and painful skin nodules. This acute presentation points towards an inflammatory process.
- **Specific Findings:**
    - **Fever:** Systemic inflammatory response.
    - **Bilateral Ankle Pain/Swelling (Polyarthritis):** Migratory polyarthritis, often affecting large joints like ankles, is characteristic.
    - **Erythema Nodosum:** Painful, erythematous nodules on anterior shins are a key feature.
    - **Chest Radiograph:** Prominent, symmetric bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy is a hallmark finding. Absence of parenchymal infiltrates is typical for Löfgren syndrome.
    - **Negative Tuberculin Skin Test (PPD):** Helps rule out tuberculosis as the primary cause, although sarcoidosis can sometimes be associated with anergy (false negative PPD).
- **Synthesis:** The combination of acute onset fever, bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy, erythema nodosum, and migratory polyarthritis in a young Scandinavian woman strongly suggests Löfgren syndrome.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
Löfgren syndrome is an acute, self-limiting variant of sarcoidosis. Sarcoidosis is a systemic granulomatous disease of unknown etiology, characterized by the formation of non-caseating granulomas in various organs, most commonly the lungs and lymph nodes. The pathogenesis involves an exaggerated cell-mediated immune response to an unidentified antigen in genetically susceptible individuals.
- **Initiating Stimulus:** Unknown antigen (possibly environmental or infectious) triggers an immune response.
- **Immune Response:** CD4+ T helper cells (Th1 type) are activated and accumulate in affected tissues.
- **Granuloma Formation:** Activated macrophages and CD4+ T cells aggregate, leading to the formation of non-caseating granulomas. These granulomas are the pathological hallmark of sarcoidosis.
- **Clinical Manifestations in Löfgren Syndrome:**
    - **Bilateral Hilar Lymphadenopathy:** Granulomas form within the hilar lymph nodes, causing enlargement visible on chest X-ray.
    - **Erythema Nodosum:** Immune complex deposition and inflammation in the subcutaneous fat panniculitis, leading to the characteristic tender nodules.
    - **Polyarthritis:** Inflammation within the synovial membranes of joints, often migratory and affecting large joints like ankles.
    - **Fever:** Systemic inflammatory response mediated by cytokines released from activated immune cells.
- **Prognosis:** Löfgren syndrome typically has an excellent prognosis, with spontaneous resolution of symptoms and radiographic findings within 1-2 years in over 85% of cases, without significant long-term morbidity or chronic organ damage. This contrasts with chronic sarcoidosis, which can lead to pulmonary fibrosis, cardiac involvement, etc.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Tuberculosis with erythema nodosum and reactive arthritis:** Tuberculosis (TB) can cause hilar lymphadenopathy and erythema nodosum. Reactive arthritis can occur post-TB infection. However, TB typically presents with caseating granulomas (not non-caseating), often involves parenchymal lung disease (infiltrates, cavitation), and the PPD test is usually positive (though can be negative in immunosuppressed or anergic patients). The classic triad and excellent prognosis are less typical for TB.
- (B) **Systemic lupus erythematosus with panniculitis and arthritis:** SLE can cause arthritis and panniculitis (lupus panniculitis, which can resemble erythema nodosum). However, SLE typically involves other systemic features (malar rash, photosensitivity, serositis, renal involvement, positive ANA/anti-dsDNA), and hilar lymphadenopathy is not a characteristic finding. The acute presentation and specific triad are not typical for SLE.
- (C) **Lyme disease with erythema migrans and arthritis:** Lyme disease can cause arthritis (often monoarticular or oligoarticular, affecting large joints like the knee) and erythema migrans (a characteristic target-like rash, distinct from erythema nodosum). Hilar lymphadenopathy is not a feature of Lyme disease. The rash morphology and lack of hilar adenopathy make this unlikely.
- (D) **Reactive arthritis secondary to a gastrointestinal infection:** Reactive arthritis (formerly Reiter's syndrome) typically follows a gastrointestinal (e.g., Salmonella, Shigella, Campylobacter) or genitourinary (e.g., Chlamydia) infection. It presents with arthritis (often asymmetric, lower extremity), urethritis, and conjunctivitis (classic triad, though not always present). Erythema nodosum is not a typical feature, and bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy is not associated with reactive arthritis.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Löfgren syndrome is a specific, acute presentation of sarcoidosis defined by the triad of bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy, erythema nodosum, and migratory polyarthritis, typically with a favorable prognosis. Key differentials include tuberculosis, other granulomatous diseases, infections causing erythema nodosum/arthritis (like streptococcal infections, TB, fungal infections), and other inflammatory conditions like SLE or reactive arthritis. Remember the characteristic triad and excellent prognosis for Löfgren syndrome.

<a id='question-109'></a>

### Question 109: Lupus Pernio
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Lupus pernio is a specific cutaneous manifestation of sarcoidosis characterized by violaceous, indurated plaques, typically on the face, strongly associated with chronic systemic disease, particularly pulmonary fibrosis. (Lupus pernio 是结节病的一种特定皮肤表现，特征是紫红色、坚硬的斑块，通常出现在面部，与慢性全身疾病密切相关，特别是肺纤维化。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old African American woman with a 10-year history of chronic cough and exertional dyspnea presents to dermatology for evaluation of facial skin lesions. She reports that the lesions have been slowly enlarging over the past 5 years and are non-pruritic. Physical examination reveals multiple, indurated, violaceous, blue-purple raised plaques and nodules symmetrically distributed across the bridge of her nose, cheeks, and earlobes. The lesions are firm to palpation and do not blanch with pressure. A punch biopsy of a representative lesion from the nasal bridge shows dense, non-caseating epithelioid granulomas in the dermis, with occasional multinucleated giant cells and lymphocytic infiltration. Serum ACE levels are elevated. Chest X-ray shows bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy and diffuse interstitial reticular opacities. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis for the patient's facial skin lesions?

(A) Granuloma annulare
(B) Erythema nodosum
(C) Lupus erythematosus (discoid lupus)
(D) Lupus pernio
(E) Cutaneous lymphoma (mycosis fungoides)

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Demographics & History:** 52-year-old African American female with a 10-year history of chronic cough and dyspnea. This suggests a chronic systemic inflammatory condition, potentially affecting the lungs. Sarcoidosis is more common in African Americans and often presents with pulmonary symptoms.
- **Skin Lesions:** Indurated, violaceous, blue-purple raised plaques and nodules on the nose, cheeks, and earlobes. This specific morphology and distribution are highly characteristic. "Indurated" (firm) and "violaceous" (purple) are key descriptors. The location on the central face (nose, cheeks) and earlobes is classic.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Dense non-caseating epithelioid granulomas in the dermis with multinucleated giant cells and lymphocytes. This is the hallmark histological finding of sarcoidosis. Non-caseating granulomas differentiate it from tuberculosis or fungal infections (which typically have caseous necrosis).
- **Laboratory & Imaging:** Elevated serum ACE levels and chest X-ray findings (bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy, interstitial reticular opacities) are strongly suggestive of systemic sarcoidosis, particularly pulmonary involvement.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the diagnosis of the facial skin lesions, given the constellation of findings.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Sarcoidosis:** A systemic inflammatory disease characterized by the formation of non-caseating epithelioid granulomas in various organs, most commonly the lungs and lymph nodes. The exact etiology is unknown, but it involves an exaggerated T-helper cell (Th1) response to an unidentified antigen in genetically susceptible individuals.
- **Lupus Pernio:** The most specific cutaneous manifestation of sarcoidosis. It presents as chronic, indurated, violaceous plaques and nodules, typically on the central face (nose, cheeks, ears), but can also occur on the extremities. The lesions are composed of dense non-caseating granulomas in the dermis.
- **Clinical Significance:** Lupus pernio is strongly associated with chronic sarcoidosis, particularly involving the lungs (often leading to pulmonary fibrosis) and upper respiratory tract. It indicates a higher likelihood of systemic involvement and a lower chance of spontaneous remission compared to other cutaneous manifestations of sarcoidosis like erythema nodosum. The violaceous color is due to dermal vascular congestion and hemosiderin deposition within the granulomas. The induration results from the dense granulomatous infiltrate and associated fibrosis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Granuloma annulare:** Presents as annular (ring-shaped) or arcuate, skin-colored or erythematous papules and plaques, often on the extremities. Histology shows palisading necrobiotic collagen surrounded by histiocytes, not non-caseating epithelioid granulomas. The clinical appearance and histology are distinct from the vignette.
- (B) **Erythema nodosum:** Presents as tender, erythematous, subcutaneous nodules, typically on the anterior shins. It is associated with various systemic conditions, including sarcoidosis, infections, and inflammatory bowel disease. Histology shows septal panniculitis (inflammation of the fat septa), not dermal granulomas. While associated with sarcoidosis, it is not the specific lesion described (violaceous plaques on the face) and has different histology.
- (C) **Lupus erythematosus (discoid lupus):** A chronic autoimmune skin condition. Lesions are typically erythematous, scaly plaques with follicular plugging and atrophy, often leading to scarring. Common locations include the face, scalp, and ears. Histology shows interface dermatitis, lymphocytic infiltrate, and vacuolar alteration of the basal cell layer, not non-caseating granulomas. The clinical description (violaceous, indurated nodules) and histology are inconsistent with discoid lupus.
- (E) **Cutaneous lymphoma (mycosis fungoides):** A type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma primarily affecting the skin. Early stages often present as eczematous or psoriatic-like patches and plaques. Advanced stages can involve tumors and erythroderma. Histology shows atypical lymphoid infiltrates in the epidermis (Pautrier microabscesses) and dermis. The clinical appearance (violaceous, indurated nodules) and the specific histology of non-caseating granulomas are not characteristic of mycosis fungoides.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Lupus pernio is the pathognomonic cutaneous lesion of sarcoidosis, characterized by chronic, violaceous, indurated plaques on the face (especially nose, cheeks, ears), and histologically by non-caseating granulomas. It signifies chronic systemic sarcoidosis.
- **Differentials:** Remember to differentiate lupus pernio from other granulomatous skin conditions (granuloma annulare, erythema nodosum), autoimmune conditions (discoid lupus), and neoplastic conditions (cutaneous lymphoma) based on clinical morphology, location, and especially histology. Erythema nodosum is another cutaneous manifestation of sarcoidosis but presents differently (tender nodules on shins) and has different histology (septal panniculitis).

<a id='question-110'></a>

### Question 110: Ocular Sarcoidosis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Sarcoidosis is a systemic granulomatous disease that commonly affects the lungs and eyes, presenting as uveitis (anterior, posterior, or panuveitis) with characteristic findings like mutton-fat keratic precipitates, retinal vasculitis, and potential complications such as glaucoma and blindness. (沙眼病是一种全身性肉芽肿性疾病，常影响肺部和眼睛，表现为葡萄膜炎（前部、后部或全葡萄膜炎），具有特征性发现，如羊毛脂角膜沉着物、视网膜血管炎以及潜在并发症，如青光眼和失明。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 33-year-old African American male with a known history of pulmonary sarcoidosis, diagnosed 5 years ago and currently managed with low-dose oral corticosteroids, presents to the ophthalmology clinic complaining of sudden onset blurred vision, photophobia, and floaters in his right eye over the past 2 days. He also reports mild eye redness. His visual acuity in the right eye is 20/70, compared to 20/20 in the left eye. Slit-lamp examination of the right eye reveals significant anterior chamber inflammation with numerous inflammatory cells and flare. Large, irregular, yellowish-white keratic precipitates ("mutton-fat" appearance) are noted on the corneal endothelium. Posterior synechiae (adhesions between the iris and the anterior lens capsule) are present superiorly. Funduscopic examination reveals diffuse retinal vasculitis, particularly prominent periphlebitis described as "candle-wax drippings" along the superior retinal vessels. Intraocular pressure is elevated at 28 mmHg in the right eye (normal < 21 mmHg). Given the patient's history and clinical findings, what is the most likely underlying pathological process responsible for the ocular manifestations?

(A) Immune complex deposition leading to fibrinoid necrosis of retinal vessels and choroidal granulomas.
(B) Non-caseating granuloma formation involving the uveal tract, conjunctiva, and retina, driven by Th1-mediated cellular immunity.
(C) Direct viral infection of the retinal pigment epithelium causing focal areas of necrosis and inflammation, associated with characteristic "headlight in the fog" appearance.
(D) Autoantibody-mediated attack on the lens epithelium, leading to cataract formation and subsequent inflammation spreading to the anterior chamber.
(E) Deposition of birefringent calcium oxalate crystals in the vitreous humor and retina, causing severe inflammation and potential retinal detachment.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 33-year-old African American male with known pulmonary sarcoidosis. Sarcoidosis is more common in African Americans and typically presents in young to middle-aged adults. Pulmonary involvement is the most common manifestation.
- **Presenting Symptoms:** Sudden blurred vision, photophobia, floaters, eye redness. These are classic symptoms of uveitis (inflammation inside the eye).
- **Visual Acuity:** Reduced vision (20/70) in the affected eye indicates significant ocular pathology.
- **Slit-Lamp Findings:**
    - Anterior chamber inflammation (cells and flare): Indicates anterior uveitis.
    - "Mutton-fat" keratic precipitates: Large, greasy-appearing KPs are highly characteristic of granulomatous anterior uveitis, strongly suggesting sarcoidosis or tuberculosis.
    - Posterior synechiae: Adhesions between the iris and lens, a common complication of anterior uveitis.
- **Funduscopic Findings:**
    - Retinal vasculitis ("candle-wax drippings"): Periphlebitis (inflammation around retinal veins) is a hallmark of posterior uveitis, particularly in sarcoidosis.
- **Intraocular Pressure:** Elevated IOP (28 mmHg) suggests secondary glaucoma, a potential complication of chronic uveitis.
- **Overall Picture:** The combination of known systemic sarcoidosis, symptoms of uveitis, characteristic "mutton-fat" KPs (granulomatous anterior uveitis), retinal vasculitis ("candle-wax drippings" - posterior uveitis), and secondary glaucoma points strongly towards ocular sarcoidosis. The question asks for the underlying pathological process.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- Sarcoidosis is a systemic inflammatory disease characterized by the formation of non-caseating granulomas in various organs.
- The pathogenesis involves an exaggerated Th1-mediated cellular immune response to an unknown antigen in genetically susceptible individuals.
- In the eye, sarcoidosis commonly causes uveitis, affecting the anterior segment (iritis, iridocyclitis), posterior segment (choroiditis, retinitis, vasculitis), or both (panuveitis).
- The granulomatous inflammation in the anterior chamber leads to the formation of large, greasy "mutton-fat" keratic precipitates on the corneal endothelium, resulting from aggregates of epithelioid macrophages and lymphocytes.
- Posterior segment involvement often manifests as retinal vasculitis (periphlebitis, often described as "candle-wax drippings" due to cuffing of the veins with inflammatory exudates) and choroidal granulomas.
- This inflammation can lead to complications like cataracts, glaucoma (due to trabeculitis or posterior synechiae), retinal detachment, and permanent vision loss.
- The underlying pathology is the formation of these non-caseating granulomas within the uveal tract (iris, ciliary body, choroid), conjunctiva, and retina, driven by the aberrant Th1 immune response.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Immune complex deposition is characteristic of diseases like SLE or polyarteritis nodosa affecting the eye, often causing fibrinoid necrosis. While inflammation occurs in sarcoidosis, the primary pathology is granuloma formation, not immune complex deposition leading to necrosis. Choroidal granulomas *can* occur in sarcoidosis, but the *primary* mechanism is granulomatous inflammation, not immune complex deposition.
- (C): Direct viral infection (e.g., herpes simplex virus, varicella-zoster virus) causes viral retinitis. This typically presents with focal retinal necrosis, intraocular inflammation, and often a "headlight in the fog" appearance (vitritis with focal retinal lesions). While it causes inflammation, the underlying mechanism is viral cytopathic effect and immune response to the virus, not granuloma formation characteristic of sarcoidosis. The "mutton-fat" KPs are also not typical of viral retinitis.
- (D): Autoantibody-mediated attack on the lens epithelium leads to primary lens opacities (cataracts). While cataracts can be a complication of chronic uveitis (including sarcoidosis), the primary pathology causing the acute uveitis symptoms (blurred vision, photophobia, redness, anterior chamber inflammation, KPs, vasculitis) is not an autoimmune attack on the lens itself.
- (E): Deposition of birefringent calcium oxalate crystals is characteristic of oxalosis, which can be primary (rare genetic disorder) or secondary (e.g., chronic renal failure). This causes severe inflammation, often with "snowbanking" in the fundus and potential retinal detachment. This mechanism is completely unrelated to sarcoidosis, which involves granulomatous inflammation.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- Sarcoidosis is a systemic granulomatous disease that frequently involves the eyes, causing uveitis (anterior, posterior, or panuveitis). The hallmark ocular findings include "mutton-fat" keratic precipitates and retinal vasculitis ("candle-wax drippings"). The underlying pathology is non-caseating granuloma formation driven by Th1 cellular immunity.
- Key differentials for granulomatous anterior uveitis include tuberculosis, syphilis, and fungal infections. Posterior uveitis differentials include infections (toxoplasmosis, CMV, HSV/VZV), autoimmune diseases (Vogt-Koyanagi-Harada syndrome, sympathetic ophthalmia), and other inflammatory conditions. The patient's history of pulmonary sarcoidosis makes ocular sarcoidosis the most likely diagnosis.

<a id='question-111'></a>

### Question 111: Heerfordt Syndrome (Uveoparotid Fever)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Heerfordt syndrome (uveoparotid fever) is a specific manifestation of sarcoidosis characterized by uveitis, parotid gland enlargement, fever, and facial nerve palsy, caused by non-caseating granulomas affecting these tissues. (Heerfordt 综合征（葡萄膜炎腮腺热）是结节病的一种特定表现形式，其特征是葡萄膜炎、腮腺肿大、发烧和面神经麻痹，这是非干酪样肉芽肿影响这些组织所致。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old woman presents to the clinic complaining of bilateral facial fullness and difficulty eating dry foods for the past month. She also reports intermittent blurred vision and a low-grade fever (37.8°C / 100°F). On physical examination, both parotid glands are symmetrically enlarged, firm, and non-tender. She exhibits a right-sided peripheral facial droop, characterized by an inability to close her right eye and raise her right eyebrow, consistent with a lower motor neuron facial nerve palsy. Her mouth is dry, and slit-lamp examination reveals anterior uveitis (inflammation of the iris and ciliary body). A biopsy of a minor salivary gland reveals multiple non-caseating granulomas. Which of the following pathophysiological mechanisms best explains this constellation of findings?

(A) Deposition of immune complexes containing rheumatoid factor in lacrimal and salivary glands, leading to lymphocytic infiltration and glandular destruction, typically associated with Sjögren syndrome.
(B) Formation of necrotizing granulomas containing acid-fast bacilli within the parotid glands and uveal tract, resulting from mycobacterial infection, characteristic of tuberculosis.
(C) Accumulation of amorphous amyloid deposits in the parotid glands, uveal tract, and facial nerve, causing compression and inflammation, often seen in systemic amyloidosis.
(D) Aggregation of epithelioid histiocytes forming non-caseating granulomas in multiple organs, including the lacrimal and salivary glands, uveal tract, and cranial nerves, driven by Th1-mediated immune response to an unknown antigen, characteristic of sarcoidosis.
(E) Formation of caseating granulomas with central necrosis in the lungs and hilar lymph nodes, often extending to the parotid glands and uveal tract via hematogenous spread, typically associated with fungal infections like histoplasmosis.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old woman. Sarcoidosis often affects young adults, particularly women.
- **Symptoms:** Bilateral, firm, painless parotid gland enlargement (suggests salivary gland involvement), dry mouth (xerostomia, related to salivary gland dysfunction), intermittent blurred vision (suggests ocular involvement), low-grade fever (systemic inflammation).
- **Physical Exam:** Symmetrical, firm, non-tender parotid enlargement. Right-sided peripheral facial droop (lower motor neuron facial nerve palsy, indicating cranial nerve VII involvement).
- **Ocular Exam:** Anterior uveitis (inflammation of the uveal tract).
- **Biopsy:** Minor salivary gland biopsy shows non-caseating granulomas. This is the hallmark histological finding of sarcoidosis.
- **Constellation of Findings:** The combination of uveitis, parotid gland enlargement, fever, and facial nerve palsy is known as Heerfordt syndrome (uveoparotid fever), a recognized subphenotype of sarcoidosis.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the underlying pathophysiological mechanism.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Sarcoidosis Pathogenesis:** Sarcoidosis is a systemic inflammatory disease characterized by the formation of non-caseating epithelioid granulomas in various organs. The exact etiology is unknown, but it is thought to involve an exaggerated Th1-mediated immune response to an unidentified antigen (possibly environmental or infectious) in genetically susceptible individuals.
- **Granuloma Formation:** Antigen-presenting cells (APCs) present the antigen to CD4+ T helper cells, leading to their differentiation into Th1 cells. Th1 cells release cytokines like IFN-γ and TNF-α, which activate macrophages. Activated macrophages aggregate and fuse to form epithelioid histiocytes, which are the main cellular component of the granuloma. Giant cells (Langhans type) may also be present. Importantly, these granulomas are typically *non-caseating*, meaning they lack central necrosis, distinguishing them from granulomas seen in tuberculosis or fungal infections.
- **Organ Involvement in Heerfordt Syndrome:**
    - **Parotid Glands:** Granulomas infiltrate the parotid glands, causing swelling, inflammation, and potentially dysfunction leading to xerostomia.
    - **Uveal Tract:** Granulomas affect the iris, ciliary body, and choroid, causing uveitis (inflammation).
    - **Facial Nerve (CN VII):** Granulomas can infiltrate the nerve itself or compress it within the parotid gland or skull base, leading to facial nerve palsy.
    - **Systemic Symptoms:** Fever and other systemic symptoms are due to the widespread inflammatory response mediated by cytokines released from granulomas and immune cells.
- **Connecting to the Vignette:** The patient's symptoms (parotid swelling, uveitis, facial palsy, fever) and the biopsy finding (non-caseating granulomas) are classic for sarcoidosis, specifically Heerfordt syndrome. The mechanism involves the formation of these characteristic non-caseating granulomas due to a Th1-driven immune response.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A) Deposition of immune complexes containing rheumatoid factor in lacrimal and salivary glands... Sjögren syndrome:** This describes the pathophysiology of Sjögren syndrome, an autoimmune disease primarily affecting exocrine glands (salivary and lacrimal), leading to dry eyes (keratoconjunctivitis sicca) and dry mouth (xerostomia). While it causes parotid swelling and dry mouth, it typically does *not* cause uveitis, facial nerve palsy, or non-caseating granulomas. The characteristic pathology is lymphocytic infiltration (lymphoepithelial sialadenitis) and immune complex deposition, not granulomas. Rheumatoid factor is often present but not the primary pathogenic mechanism.
- **(B) Formation of necrotizing granulomas containing acid-fast bacilli... tuberculosis:** This describes the pathophysiology of tuberculosis (TB). TB granulomas are typically *caseating* (containing central necrosis) and contain acid-fast bacilli (Mycobacterium tuberculosis). While TB can affect the parotid glands and uveal tract, the granulomas are caseating, not non-caseating, and the presence of acid-fast bacilli would be expected. The vignette specifically mentions non-caseating granulomas.
- **(C) Accumulation of amorphous amyloid deposits... systemic amyloidosis:** This describes the pathophysiology of amyloidosis. Amyloid deposition can occur in various organs, including salivary glands, nerves, and the eye, but it does not typically form granulomas. The pathology involves extracellular deposition of misfolded proteins (amyloid fibrils), not inflammatory granulomas. While amyloidosis can cause organ dysfunction, the clinical picture and biopsy findings in the vignette are inconsistent with amyloidosis.
- **(E) Formation of caseating granulomas with central necrosis... fungal infections like histoplasmosis:** This describes the pathophysiology of granulomatous infections like histoplasmosis or coccidioidomycosis. These infections typically cause *caseating* granulomas, similar to tuberculosis, due to central necrosis. While they can affect multiple organs, including the lungs, lymph nodes, and potentially disseminate to other sites, the granulomas are caseating, not non-caseating, and the specific constellation of Heerfordt syndrome is not characteristic of these infections.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Heerfordt syndrome (uveoparotid fever) is a classic presentation of sarcoidosis, characterized by uveitis, parotid enlargement, fever, and facial nerve palsy, caused by non-caseating granulomas. Recognize this specific clinical pattern and its underlying pathology.
- **Differentials:** Consider Sjögren syndrome (dry glands, but no uveitis/facial palsy/granul
